
######## LENS: TRAVEL, ARRIVAL, DEATH AND SOFTLOCKS
NOTES: HARNESS, READ THIS FIRST: port 8492 does NOT serve this worktree. It serves .claude/worktrees/suspicious-driscoll-00fbd8, whose comp/ninth.js is 15,059 lines against this worktree's 8,279 and is being edited live (md5 changed between two curls a minute apart). It has a whole camera punch/fx layer that does not exist here. Every capture taken with the brief's documented 8492 URL is a capture of a different game. I served this worktree myself on 8493 with .claude/nnqc/trav-server.js (node, 20 lines, no deps): `node .claude/nnqc/trav-server.js`, then the same canvas.html URLs with 8493. This worktree's comp/ninth.js is byte-identical to the main repo root's.
Exit graph, walked by hand and re-derived in node for all 19 exits and all 18 reachable (from,to) pairs (.claude/nnqc/trav-graph.js): every arrival is standable, none is inside a prop, none is inside any exit band, none is outside moveActor's clamp. The graph is strongly connected apart from a3sq, which is oneway:1 and correctly declared.
Exit band shapes: no exit in the game sets both w and h, and none sets neither, so exitAt's 0.9 defaults are never both used. Every band's inner 72% or more lies inside the walk clamp; the outer part of an edge band hangs past the floor, which is finding [18]'s clamp mismatch and not a reachability problem.
The armed flag is genuinely dead weight today rather than a defect: no arrival in the game lands in a band, so exitAt returns null on the first live frame and armed flips true immediately. It is correct defensive code; it just never fires.
gotoPlace's reset block plus the six onPlaceChange registrations (combat rep/cuts/encI/lull at 2793, the act-3 block at 5530, a3After at 5545, RT.world.cut at 6665, job 5's freeSlant/atShop/fillBag at 8257, the travel sfx at 8271) cover everything I could find that must not cross a doorway, with the single exception of RT.mapOpen, reported. RT.panel also survives and stepTravel does not check it, so you can walk out of a place with the book open — that is the accepted 'panel() pauses nothing' note in PARALLEL.md and I am not counting it. RT.echo, RT.silence, RT.stanzaCd, RT.iframe and RT.casting also survive; all read as deliberate.
Thrash: 50 place changes across all 13 places with a Mouth, a Thief and a Droner spawned each time, Calls and Answers in flight, a dialogue opened every third trip, the map every fourth, a panel every fifth, and 16 keys mashed through the real keydown chain before each transition. Zero console errors, zero window.onerror, zero place mismatches, zero NaN positions. End state clean (foes 0, fproj 0, calls 0, timers 0). .claude/nnqc/trav-probe.html section 3.
Close-and-reopen from all 13 places: all recover, all land on (W/2, H-2.2), all standable, no throws. Reload into a3sq in all four ending states: written and silence both come home to the square at (8.70,7.80); true waits for R and then comes home; no ending state strands the save.
The gated doors survive everything: rehearsed, chorusDown and a3ready are all S.seen keys and therefore saved, and the loft (the only room you can be locked inside) sets chorusDown from foeDie's BOSS_DOWN hook, which no death, reload or reset can skip. Dying in the loft respawns you at (6.5,11) with the Chorus still standing and its damage intact — no reset, no lockout.
The stage is not a trap despite having no exits: SCRIPTS.prologue's beat(13.4) is the only way out, and the only things that clear RT.beats are gotoPlace itself and two dev buttons that also travel. Nothing can kill you there, so the dead branch cannot freeze the queue.
`oneway` is the same class of field as `dark:1` in [5] — read only by the audit, never by the game — but the audit's use of it is legitimate and its semantics are spelled out in the message, so I am not counting it as a second instance.
PARALLEL.md is stale in two more ways my lens touched: 'gotoPlace fully heals the player and refills Breath on every transition' is now only half true (comp/ninth.js:6153-6155 heals fully in calm/mends places and clamps to 35% elsewhere; breath is still refilled unconditionally everywhere), and `calm` is not a dead field — it is read at 6153 and 6161.
Scratch, all under .claude/nnqc/ and nothing under comp/ touched: trav-server.js (the 8493 server), trav-graph.js (node derivation of every arrival, revive point and band), trav-probe.html (death state, thrash, close/reopen, a3 states), trav-probe2.html (death with the clock run out, winded, the square script, shut doors, double-E), trav-probe3.html (real-travel-only arrivals and scripts), trav-probe4.html (a3sq exit routes in wall-clock-synced time), trav-shot.html (capture page with an hcase param). Screenshots: trav-roaddeath-f0.png, trav-roaddeath-f39.png, trav-winded2.png, trav-wickagain2.png, trav-shutdoor.png.

  title: revive() refills the breath bar but leaves you winded, so you get up with a full bar that reads WINDED and no verbs for 1.5 of your 1.6 invulnerable seconds
  where: comp/ninth.js:1786 revive(), against comp/ninth.js:6155 gotoPlace; RT.winded is ticked only at comp/ninth.js:1717 inside stepPlayer, which step() only calls on the live branch (comp/ninth.js:3827-3836)
  what : gotoPlace does the pair on one line: `RT.breath = stats().breathMax; RT.winded = 0;`. revive() copies only the first half: `RT.breath = stats().breathMax` with no winded reset. Worse, the lockout does not even run down while you are dead. `RT.winded -= dt` lives at comp/ninth.js:1717 inside stepPlayer, and step() branches at 3823 so stepPlayer is not called at all while RT.dead. downPlayer sets RT.deadT = 2.2 (comp/ninth.js:1779) and windedT is 1.5 (TUNE, comp/ninth.js:160), so a lockout that should have expired twice over during the death animation is instead frozen and handed back to you intact. Measured: die on the frame you go winded and you revive with winded = 1.50 exactly, against RT.iframe = 1.6. Every verb reads it: doCall (2378), doSwallow (2406), doRhyme (2558), doStanza (2891) and spendBreath itself (2161) all return on `RT.winded > 0`, each with a hudNudge that shakes the bar. So 94% of the one window the game gives you to reposition after a death is a window in which you cannot Call, cannot Answer, cannot press a rhyme and cannot recite, while updateHud (comp/ninth.js:4692) paints the word WINDED across a bar that is visibly full. Reachable on the main path: going winded is exactly the state the game kills you in — spendBreath's own slam says 'doubled over, and everything can see you' — and the Chorus fight in the loft is where it happens.
  repro: http://localhost:8493/.claude/nnqc/trav-shot.html?dev=ninth&hcase=windeddeath&hafter=2&hz=2&hx=0&hy=270 -> .claude/nnqc/trav-winded2.png: the breath bar is filled edge to edge and lettered WINDED, one frame after 'Take it from the top.' Numbers from .claude/nnqc/trav-probe2.html section 2 (real Mouths do the damage, the death clock is run out in full): `revived after 2.22s: breath=40/40 winded=1.50 iframe=1.60 HUD="WINDED" / doCall while "WINDED" over a full bar: calls in flight 0 -> 0, breath still 40`. Control on the same page: set winded and walk through a door instead and it is `winded=0 breath=40`.
  fix  : One clause, in step with the line gotoPlace already has: comp/ninth.js:1786 becomes `RT.breath = stats().breathMax; RT.winded = 0;`. If the lockout is meant to survive a death, then it has to keep running during it, which means moving the `RT.winded -= dt` out of stepPlayer and up next to the other RT.dead-agnostic clocks at the top of step().

      Mechanism verified exactly, in source and in a live run.

SOURCE:
- comp/ninth.js:1785-1786 revive() is `RT.dead = false; RT.hp = RT.hpm; RT.breath = stats().breathMax; RT.iframe = 1.6;` with no RT.winded reset. comp/ninth.js:6155 gotoPlace is `RT.breath = stats().breathMax; RT.winded = 0; RT.dead = false;`. The asymmetry is real.
- grep for `winded` across the file: it is written at 351 (item breath), 371 (item horn), 999 (dev button), 1549 (init), 2166 (spendBreath sets it to T('windedT')), 6155 (gotoPlace). It is DECREMENTED in exactly one place, comp/ninth.js:1717, the first statement of stepPlayer.
- comp/ninth.js:3822-3836 step(): `if (RT.dead) { RT.deadT -= (real||dt); stepParts(dt); if (RT.deadT <= 0) revive(); } else { stepPlayer(dt); ... }`. stepPlayer is not on the dead branch, so line 1717 is unreachable while dead. stepPlayer's own `if (RT.dead) return;` is at 1730, AFTER the winded tick, which is the guard that would have covered this had step() still called it. It does not.
- Numbers: TUNE.windedT = 1.5 (line 160), downPlayer sets RT.deadT = 2.2 (line 1779), revive sets RT.iframe = 1.6 (line 1786). 2.2 > 1.5, so a running lockout would always have expired during the death.
- Verb gates confirmed by reading each: spendBreath 2161 `if (RT.winded > 0) return false;`; doCall 2378, doSwallow 2406, doRhyme 2558, doStanza 2891, each `if (RT.winded > 0) { hudNudge('breath'); return; }`. doAnswer (2717) has no gate of its own but tail-calls doRhyme at 2724, so it is cov
      CORRECTION: revive() (comp/ninth.js:1786) refills breath but does not reset RT.winded, and the lockout does not run down during death because its only decrement (comp/ninth.js:1717) lives in stepPlayer, which step()'s dead branch (comp/ninth.js:3822-3826) never calls. So a player who dies while winded is revived with the remaining lockout intact on a full breath bar: the HUD paints a 100%-wide bar lettered WINDED (comp/ninth.js:4688-4692, comp.css:2945), and doCall/doSwallow/doRhyme/doAnswer/doStanza all refuse with a bar shake for the remainder. Two corrections to the finding as written: the residual is 1.5 minus the time already survived while winded, so 1.50s is the ceiling (reachable only by dying on the exact frame you go winded), not the typical case; a natural death measured 0.267s. And repositioning is NOT lost: doDash (comp/ninth.js:1755) has no winded check and works during the whole window, as does WASD movement. What is lost is offence during up to 1.5s of the 1.6s of post-revive invulnerability. The suggested one-clause fix at 1786 is still correct.

"winded = 1.50 against iframe 1.60, so 94% of the window" only happens if you die on the same frame you go winded.   what : gotoPlace snaps the eye on arrival and says in its own comment why. revive() is the game's other teleport and it leaves the camera to ease. Die anywhere at the north end and the camera is pinned at (-177, 20). revive() drops you at (pw()/2, ph()-2) = (5.5, 32), which projects to base (isoXB -208.5, isoYB+TILE_H/2 642.25), i.e. canvas (-31.5, 622.25) against a 1120x580 canvas: 31px past the left edge and 42px below the bottom edge. You are not on screen at all on the frame you get up. The camera's new clamped target is (-490, 250), 388px away, and the ease is `k = 1 - pow(0.0016, dt)` = 0.1018 per frame at 60Hz, so it takes 0.65s to get within 5px and 0.93s to get within 1px — most of the 1.6s of i-frames, spent looking at empty road. Every other place is small enough that the two clamps coincide and the pan is 0-7px, so this is the road specifically, which is also the place a player is most likely to be killed while walking.

But the visual claim is wrong: you are never "not on screen at all" for any perceptible length of time, and the road is never "empty".

revive() is called from step() at comp/ninth.js:3826, and step() falls through to the shared eased `stepCamera(real || dt)` at comp/ninth.js:3838 BEFORE draw() runs. So the camera has already taken one ease step by the first frame that is drawn. The finding computes the screen position at n=0 eases (feet -31.5, 622.25), which is a state no frame is ever drawn in.

Measured on the true revive frame (probe .claude/nnqc/sk-revcam.html, which stops on the tick that flips RT.dead false and does not add a trailing tick):
  revive+0  cam -208.84,43.40  feet   0.34,598.85
  revive+1  cam -237.45,64.42  feet  28.95,577.83
  revive+2  cam -263.14,83.30  feet  54.64,558.95
blit (comp/ninth.js:1864) anchors x-centred, y at the feet, so the 32x40 sprite box on revive+0 is x[-15.7,16.3] y[558.8,598.8]: 16.3 of 32 px wide and 21.2 of 40 px tall are on the 1120x580 canvas, i.e. 27% of the sprite (head and right shoulder), plus the 120px lantern radial gradient drawn at comp/ninth.js:2144. drawActor is pushed into ents unconditionally at comp/ninth.js:3889 with no off-screen cull, so it is drawn. By revive+1 (16ms) the whole sprite is on canvas; .claude/nnqc/sk-revcam-z8-f1.png shows the figure plainly at 8x zoom on exactly the state the finding's own screenshot is of.

The finding's screenshot and title readings are one frame later than labelled: trav-shot.html line 71 runs `N().tick(1, 1/60)` after the `while (R.dead)` loop, so hafter=0 is revive+1. Its own reported `screen 29,578` is on-canvas and contradicts its prose "You are not on screen at all on the frame you get up". The f0 PNG was read at full-canvas scale where a dim i-framed sprite (alpha oscillates 0.20-0.90 per comp/ninth.js:2116) in the bottom-left corner is easy to miss.

"most of the 1.6s of i-frames, spent looking at empty road" is also overstated by its own arithmetic: 0.65s of 1.6s is 41%, and the player is visible for all of it.

What is left is a real inconsistency: gotoPlace states the rule ("the eye arrives with you, it does not pan in from the last place") and revive, the game's other teleport, breaks it on the one place where the two camera clamps do not coincide. After dying anywhere with py <= 14.3 on the road you stand in the bottom-left corner while the eye sweeps back down the road for about 0.65s. That is a camera-polish inconsistency, not a broken frame. The suggested one-line fix is correct and would be a no-op for every other place.

[N7] UNVERIFIED (ran out of budget) (risk) sevVotes=[]
  title: RT.prompt survives the doorway it just opened, so a second E in the same frame re-enters the place you are already in and teleports you to its default arrival point
  where: comp/ninth.js:6249-6251 doInteract (`else if (o.k === 'exit') { ... gotoPlace(o.e.to, false); }`) against comp/ninth.js:1749-1750, where stepTravel and then `RT.prompt = nearestInteract()` run in that order inside stepPlayer
  what : stepTravel's own gotoPlace is safe: it is called from inside stepPlayer at 1749 and line 1750 immediately refreshes RT.prompt against the new place. doInteract is not — it runs from the keydown handler (comp/ninth.js:1688), outside the frame, and neither doInteract nor gotoPlace clears RT.prompt. So between the E that travels and the next stepPlayer, RT.prompt is still the old place's exit object, and a second E takes it. `gotoPlace(o.e.to)` with `o.e.to === RT.place` then finds no back exit (no place has an exit to itself), falls to the else branch at comp/ninth.js:6144 and sets RT.px = W/2, RT.py = H-2.2 — a teleport. It also replays bigLine(p.n), re-runs the whole RESETS chain (which includes poemStash() and fillLine(true), i.e. it deals you a fresh hand of words mid-frame), clears RT.foes/fproj/calls/beats/timers a second time, and re-runs the place script for anywhere that has one. RT.armed is false at that point so stepTravel cannot compound it, and E is auto-repeat-filtered at comp/ninth.js:1683, so this needs two genuine key presses inside one frame period. That is 16.7ms at 60Hz — hard by hand, but the frame right after a place change is the most expensive frame in the game (round one measured 11.6-23.7ms for the first frame in), and it is free on any machine dropping to 30Hz.
  repro: .claude/nnqc/trav-probe2.html section 6 dispatches real keydown/keyup events with no step() between them, standing at (8.50,13.50) in the square: `after E#1: place=lane pos 6.20,14.80` (the correct back-exit arrival) then `after E#2 (no step between): place=lane pos 6.50,14.80` — 6.5 is lane W/2 and 14.8 is lane H-2.2, i.e. the default branch fired and moved the player. The stale object is printed too: `RT.prompt still points at -> lane`.
  fix  : `RT.prompt = null;` at the top of gotoPlace, next to `RT.moveTo = null; RT.armed = false; RT.nagged = null;` at comp/ninth.js:6145. stepPlayer rebuilds it on the next frame anyway, and drawPrompt already no-ops on a null prompt. Belt and braces: `if (id === RT.place) return;` as the first line of gotoPlace would also make every double-fire in the file harmless, but it would break a3Home's deliberate square-to-square call, so the prompt reset is the right one.

[N9] UNVERIFIED (ran out of budget) (risk) sevVotes=[]
  title: Audit hole: the audit models gotoPlace's arrival exactly and does not know revive() exists, which is the one teleport in the game with no unstick() behind it
  where: .claude/ninth-night/tools/audit-geometry.js:236-254 (the arrival block), against comp/ninth.js:1787 revive() and comp/ninth.js:6147 gotoPlace's `unstick()`
  what : The audit reproduces gotoPlace's arithmetic line for line at audit-geometry.js:241-247 and warns on 'arriving from X lands inside a prop', adding 'unstick() should catch it, but do not rely on that.' That is a faithful model of one of the three places the engine teleports the player, and the only one that actually has unstick() behind it. It does not model the other two. (1) revive() sets RT.px = pw()/2, RT.py = ph()-2 (comp/ninth.js:1787) and then calls nothing: no unstick(), no clamp, no exit-band test, and RT.armed is left true. Today all 13 spawn points are clear — I checked each against every prop at r 0.3 — but the margins are thin and unwarned: the nearest exit-band edge is 0.85 tiles in square, bernhouse, chandler, lane, mill, loft, road and hollow, so an exit authored one tile further in would drop a respawning player straight into a live band with armed already true and bounce them out of the place they died in, and a prop authored across the middle of a place's south third would leave them inside it with no recovery, since blocked() only ever tests a destination and never notices you are already inside something. (2) init/close-and-reopen uses the else branch for every place, not just a3sq: init sets RT.place = S.place at comp/ninth.js:1555 and then calls gotoPlace(S.place) at 1576, so prev === id, no back exit is ever found, and the reload spawn is (W/2, H-2.2) in all thirteen. The audit only evaluates that branch for a place some other place points at with no way back, i.e. a3sq alone, so twelve reload spawns are unchecked. Round one's note that '(W/2, H-2.2) is used by exactly one place, a3sq' is true of walking and not of loading.

    [['respawn after a death', W / 2, H - 2], ['loading the save here', W / 2, H - 2.2]].forEach(function (s) {
        var px = s[1], py = s[2];
        if (px < 0.5 || py < 0.5 || px > W - 0.5 || py > H - 0.5)
            bad(id + ': ' + s[0] + ' puts you at ' + px + ',' + py + ', outside the walk clamp.');
        if (blocked(p, px, py, R))
            bad(id + ': ' + s[0] + ' puts you inside a prop at ' + px + ',' + py + '. revive() does not call unstick().');
        (p.exits || []).forEach(function (e) {
            var w = e.w || 0.9, h = e.h || 0.9;
            if (px > e.x - w / 2 && px < e.x + w / 2 && py > e.y - h / 2 && py < e.y + h / 2)

[N10] UNVERIFIED (ran out of budget) (polish) sevVotes=[]
  title: After the true ending the only way out of a square with no exits is the R key, and the line that says so fades after 5.2 seconds
  where: comp/ninth.js:5868-5873 a3True and comp/ninth.js:5654-5661 a3Resume; PLACES.a3sq has `exits: []` at comp/ninth.js:5288; say()'s fade timer at the `say` helper (5200ms, then 700ms to remove)
  what : a3sq has no exits by design and a3Resume's comment says the right thing: 'anything that returns without putting the player somewhere is a permanent softlock that survives a reload.' Two of the three endings honour that — written and silence both run a3Credits into a3Home, and I confirmed both come home on a cold reload. The true ending does not: a3True's last beat lights S.verse, nudges the Verse button and says '<i>R</i>', and then nothing else is queued. RT.story.done is 1 so a3Watch has returned; there are no beats left and no exits; the only transition out of a3sq is doVerse -> a3Hal -> a3Credits -> a3Home. I confirmed pressing R does work and does get you home to the square at (8.70,7.80), and that the state is recoverable across a reload. What is thin is the affordance: the '<i>R</i>' line is an ordinary say(), so 5.9 seconds later it has faded out of the DOM, RT.prompt is null (there is nothing in the place to interact with), the place title has gone, and what is left on the last screen of the game is a lit Verse button in the bottom right corner. Better, since a3sq is the one place with no exits: push a fake interactable so the world layer's own prompt does the work, e.g. have interactables() return a `{ k: 'verse', x: RT.px, y: RT.py + 2, label: 'sing the last verse' }` when RT.place === 'a3sq' && S.verse && !S.a3.verseSpent, and have doInteract call doVerse on it. Then the last screen of the game has the same affordance as every other screen.

The stuck-bail in stepNpcs never fires spuriously on overshoot. dt is clamped to 0.05 at comp/ninth.js:3804 and scaled by timeScale*dilation, so the fastest step any NPC takes is 1.9*0.05 = 0.095 tiles against an arrival radius of 0.16; overshoot would need sp > 2d - 0.001 = 0.319. The `bob: RT.t * 2.4` fallback at :7955 is dead, every NPC gets an id at :5470.

The adult walk cycle is on a fixed clock, not on distance: bob advances at 2.4 rad/s regardless of speed, so a leg pose lasts pi/2.4 = 1.31 s. The shepherd covers 1.11 tiles per pose and the chandler 0.92, which reads acceptably; a walker authored without `speed` would use the 1.5 default and cover 1.96 tiles per pose, which would glide. Latent only, so noting rather than reporting.
NPCs are not solid — blocked() reads place().props only — so you can stand inside Bern. That looks like a deliberate choice rather than a defect, but it is worth knowing that it is not enforced anywhere.

[N11] UNVERIFIED (ran out of budget) (bug) sevVotes=[]
  title: Every person is mirrored on a world-space test, so a quarter of all walking is drawn facing backwards — and one whole leg of the child's loop is backwards 100% of the time
  where: comp/ninth.js:6206 stepNpcs `w.face = dx >= 0 ? 1 : -1` against comp/ninth.js:7978 drawNpc `if (w2.face < 0) cx.scale(-1, 1)`. Same mistake at comp/ninth.js:2113 drawActor `var west = Math.cos(RT.face) < 0 ? -1 : 1` with the scale at :2118 and RT.face built at :1751.
  what : `cx.scale(-1,1)` is a mirror in SCREEN x. Screen x is isoX = ORX + (x - y)*29, so the screen direction of travel is sign(dx - dy), not sign(dx). Both sites test sign(dx) alone. Writing dx = cos t, dy = sin t, dx - dy = sqrt(2)*cos(t + 45deg): the two signs disagree for t in (45,90) and (225,270), i.e. 90 of 360 degrees, 25% of all directions, and the disagreement is exactly the case where somebody walks mostly along +y or -y (which on screen is left or right, not up or down).

Measured in the real game, 60 s of real step()+draw() frames per walker, comparing w.face against the actual per-frame change in (x - y):
  child     1133 of 2552 walking frames drawn backwards = 44.4%
  chandler   328 of 1273 = 25.8%
  shepherd    25.1% (over 40 runs x 300 s offline; the 60 s live sample was 11.5%, too few legs to be stable)

The child is worst for a deterministic reason. Her leg from path[2] (7.4,12.6) to path[3] (7.4,10.0) has dx = 0 authored, but she always ARRIVES at path[2] from the east and stops the first frame d < 0.16, so her x is always slightly greater than 7.4. dx is therefore a tiny negative number for the whole next leg while dy is -2.6, so face = -1 while (dx - dy) = +2.6: she walks 2.6 tiles up-and-right across the screen, for 1.37 s, mirrored, once every loop. Offline per-leg counts: 116253 of 116253 frames on that leg wrong, 0 of 140407 on the leg before it. On top of that she slides along the well blocked in x (see the next finding) while face is still taken from the target, so she moves LEFT down the screen drawn facing right for another ~0.8 s.

It matters visually because the mirror moves the carried prop 26 px across the body: NPC_PROP (comp/ninth.js:2081) hangs the shepherd's crook at dx +13, the widow's lamp at +13, the busker's lute at -13. Also GESTURE (comp/ninth.js:7914) is drawn AFTER the flip's restore at :7993, so a person who stops facing west whittles or reaches with the hand on the mirrored side.

The player has the same line. RT.face is a world-space angle (correct, doCall/doDash aim with it) but `west` reduces it with cos alone. So the character faces away from the cursor whenever the cursor is up-and-right at a screen slope steeper than 1:2, or down-and-left at the same slope.
  repro: Shepherd, two runs identical except the target moves 0.8 tiles in world x, both of which walk him up-and-RIGHT on screen:
  .claude/nnqc/pplq-shepA.png  target (9.6,4.6) -> face +1, crook on his screen-right, sx 682 -> 701
  .claude/nnqc/pplq-shepB.png  target (8.8,4.6) -> face -1, crook on his screen-LEFT,  sx 682 -> 695
Player, cursor to the RIGHT of him both times:
  .claude/nnqc/pplq-pcA.png  aim world (9.0,6.5) from (8,8): cursor +72.5 px screen-right, lantern drawn on the right
  .claude/nnqc/pplq-pcB.png  aim world (7.5,6.0) from (8,8): cursor +43.5 px screen-right, lantern drawn on the LEFT
All four from .claude/nnqc/pplq-probe.html (hosts the real game, sets a walker's target, then runs the real step loop). Counts from .claude/nnqc/pplq-face.js and the probe's trace mode.
  fix  : comp/ninth.js:6206 -> `w.face = (dx - dy) >= 0 ? 1 : -1;`
comp/ninth.js:2113 -> `var west = (Math.cos(RT.face) - Math.sin(RT.face)) < 0 ? -1 : 1;` (leave RT.face itself alone, it is the aim vector). Optionally move the GESTURE call at :7999 inside the flip so an idle gesture mirrors with the body.

  title: Everything drawn inside drawNpc is under the night veil: the talk mark is three pixels and the widow's lamp is the only light in the game at half strength
  where: comp/ninth.js:8002-8004 (the '·' mark) and comp/ninth.js:7985-7991 (the carried-lamp glow), both inside drawNpc which runs in the ents pass at comp/ninth.js:3891, against drawLights' full-screen veil at comp/ninth.js:7834-7836 called at :3892 and drawPrompt at :3902.
  what : draw() runs the ents pass, then drawLights, which opens with a flat `fillRect(0,0,VW,VH)` of rgba(6,5,14,0.5) outdoors (0.66 in the hollow, rgba(14,8,10,.34) indoors), then paints every prop light over the top with 'lighter'. So the two overlays drawNpc owns are the only light-coloured marks in the world layer that get exactly half their contrast taken away, while drawPrompt — which carries the same 'you can talk to this' information — is drawn after drawVignette and loses none.

Measured present-vs-absent on the real canvas, same frame, NPC moved out of the place as the control:
  the mark over Bern    12x10 px box: 47.38 with him, 46.39 without -> +0.99 mean, i.e. about 3 lit pixels

Two smaller things in the same three lines. (a) The widow's lamp is not in lightsOf at all (comp/ninth.js:7812-7828 reads p.props and p.lights only), so unlike all seven prop lamps it is a true circle glued to the sprite rather than a `scale(1,0.62)` pool on the ground: the woman whose entire character is setting a lamp down carries the one lamp in Wick that never touches the floor. [N15] UNVERIFIED (ran out of budget) (risk) sevVotes=[]
  title: NPCs collide at r 0.28 and clamp to 0.6 while the player collides at r 0.3 and clamps to 0.5, so eight gaps in the game admit a walker and are sealed to you
  where: comp/ninth.js:6203-6205 stepNpcs (`blocked(nx, w.y, 0.28)`, `clamp(w.x, 0.6, pw() - 0.6)`) against comp/ninth.js:6053-6057 moveActor (`r = r || 0.3`, `clamp(nx, 0.5, W - 0.5)`); blocked() comp/ninth.js:6044-6050; unstick() comp/ninth.js:6082 is player-only.
  what : blocked() grows each AABB by the mover's radius, so a corridor of width g admits the player only if g > 0.6 and an NPC if g > 0.56. Scanning every prop pair in every place, the game contains exactly eight gaps of width 0.600 and none in between:
  bernhouse  wall/bed 0.600, wall/shelf 0.600, wall/crate 0.600
  chandler   wall/shelf 0.600 (x2), wall/vat 0.600, wall/shelf 0.600
  mill       sack/sack 0.600
At exactly 0.600 the player's admissible set is the single exact coordinate x = X1 + 0.3, which floating-point movement will never land on (round one's collision lens already met one of these as the 'zero-width pocket' in bernhouse). The NPC's admissible set is 0.04 tiles wide — thin, but real. The clamps disagree in the same direction: the road's east verge is 0.8..0.88 wide beside its fence and post, standable for the player and not for an NPC.

Nothing goes wrong today: I replayed the three walkers over 1200 s each and none of them comes within reach of any of the eight, and the deepest any of them ever gets from a standable player cell is 0.12 tiles (child 0.08, shepherd 0.06, chandler 0.12) against a 1.9-tile talk radius, so everybody is always talkable. It goes live the first time a walker is authored near one of those gaps, and it is unrecoverable when it does, because stepNpcs' give-up only abandons the leg — there is no unstick() for NPCs, and blocked() tests the destination only, so it never notices somebody is already inside something. This is the same shape as round one [22] (which is about the clamp order) at a different site: [22] is 'the clamp can push a walker into a prop', this is 'a walker can walk into a slot the player cannot follow it into'.
  repro: node .claude/nnqc/pplq-struct.js sections A and B. Section A enumerates the eight gaps from PLACES; section B replays each walker for 1200 s and reports the worst distance to a standable player cell.
  fix  : Make the two agree: use 0.3 and 0.5..size-0.5 for NPCs too (one edit each at comp/ninth.js:6203, :6204 and :6205), so anywhere a person can stand you can stand. If the 0.28 is deliberate slack for doorways, add the check to audit-geometry.js instead: warn on any prop-pair or prop-to-clamp gap in [0.56, 0.62] within `wander` of an NPC's home or within 0.5 tiles of any path segment.

######## LENS: the building painters themselves — every PAINT.* and material helper read for the one light rule, raw anti-aliased ctx calls, footprint-quad registration, anchors, and paintedBox/SPRITE_PAD, with the sprites measured pixel by pixel
NOTES: HARNESS WARNING FOR THE NEXT AGENT: the dev server on port 8492 is NOT serving this worktree. It serves a tree whose comp/ninth.js is 14012 lines against our 8279 (same PAINT.cart byte for byte, so it is a much-further-along branch), and /.claude/nnqc/ 404s there. Every capture I took against it came back as a blank 11KB PNG. I ran `python -m http.server 8571` rooted at this worktree instead and verified the served ninth.js md5 matches comp/ninth.js before any capture. Every URL in the findings above is written against 8571.
Second harness note: canvas.html's hw= lands the target at 25%/25% of the shot, not the centre, because it assumes a 600x310 CSS window and the documented capture is 1200x620. Shift the aim point left by 300/z and up by 155/z canvas px to actually centre a prop.
Third: the sprite LRU zeroes cv.width on eviction, and the game's sprites total 12.07MB against a 10MB budget (round one's [25]), so a probe that walks all thirteen places and measures afterwards silently loses the stage. Measure per place, or copy the canvas.

THE ONE LIGHT RULE, prop by prop. Every painter that reaches for pal[] gets the indices right: body() is the reference (sw = pal[0] mid, se = pal[1] dark, top = pal[2] light) and every pal override passed to it is written [mid, dark, light] — wheel, markstone, post, cart, foot, bed all check out numerically, as does every entry in the PROP table. plaster is called (sw, true) / (se, false) in both PAINT.house and PAINT.wall; PAINT.mill runs `[[sw, true], [se, false]]`; PAINT.hearth runs `[[f.sw, true], [f.se, false]]`; PAINT.markstone lightens f.sw and darkens f.se; PAINT.well's `lit = sx < rrx * 0.2` lights the left 60% of the ring; PAINT.barrel lights the left 72% of every slice and darkens the right 56%; PAINT.lamp lights the left half of the post; PAINT.tree lights the left 42% of the trunk and offsets every canopy highlight up-left; roundBody and cylBody both put the highlight left and the sky on top; PAINT.hedge lightens each pass as it lifts. The ONLY inversion in the file is the roof of a flip house (finding 1). Nothing swaps pal[0] and pal[1] anywhere.

Anchors: every anchor a LIVE reads is set by the painter that owns it, and none fall through anchorAt's (0,0). LIVE.house 'chimney' <- 7006, LIVE.mill 'hoist' <- 7095, LIVE.wheel 'hub' <- 7140, LIVE.hearth 'fire' <- 7744. LIVE.lamp and LIVE.foot use no anchor: LIVE.lamp's flame at myc-hgt-12 spans -hgt-16..-hgt-8 and PAINT.lamp's glazing is -hgt-18..-hgt-7, so it is inside the lantern; LIVE.foot rebuilds the strip from o.b and c.x3 = -(bw+bh)*TILE_W/4 is identically isoX(b0, b1+b3) - mxc, so it lands on the painted reflectors. All confirmed, and round one had already checked the last two. The one anchor that is set to the wrong PLACE is 'fire' (finding 4) — it is read correctly, it is just written five pixels below the floor.

The brief is stale on two props: cairn and counter DO have bespoke painters. `PAINT.stone = PAINT.cairn` at comp/ninth.js:7468 (and the cairn branch at 7485-7491 stacks three more stones on top of the base one) and `PAINT.table = PAINT.counter` at comp/ninth.js:7619 (with a counter-only branch at 7634-7645 for the scales, the shadow line under the lip and the open ledger). Nothing in PLACES falls through to PAINT._ ; every one of the 27 types in the PROP table has an entry in PAINT except `_` itself. The generic solid is unreachable in the shipped world.

Registration, everything else clean: PAINT.fence's `+ TILE_H / 2` at 7317 is still the only local coordinate patch in the file (round one's [20], already covered). PAINT.foot's nine reflectors run along the real SW edge c.x3->c.x2 at both authored sizes. PAINT.stagewip's scaffolding walks qp(f.top, q, 0.5), which is the long axis for both the 4.6x2.6 and the 8x3.2 instance. PAINT.hedge's run resolves to exactly the footprint's long centre line (its x offset is right, only its y is wrong). PAINT.curtain, PAINT.shelf, PAINT.crate, PAINT.table/counter, PAINT.bed, PAINT.beam and PAINT.post all take their points off f.sw / f.top / f.se via qp and bar and sit on their quads. bar() is used correctly in all four of its call sites.

WEAKEST THREE, asked for explicitly. 1) wheel — a 128x70 untextured solid with an anti-aliased vector circle drawn on it every frame; it is a shed with a wheel painted on the side, and it is the mill's one working part (finding 7). 2) cart — one wheel on the tailgate fifteen pixels off the ground and 81% of its rail invisible against its own bed (finding 3). 3) stagewip — the biggest prop in the game (8x3.2 in a3sq, 650x325 screen px) and its SE face is never touched at all, so it keeps body()'s flat pal[1] over roughly 2400 px², while its top face gets twelve 2px marks and a 10% dither and no boards. Every other flat-topped prop lays board seams on both. That last one is on the line between a defect and taste, so I have left it here rather than as a finding — but it is the surface the whole third act is played on.

Also seen and deliberately not reported: comp/ninth.js:7448 is `[[c.x3, c.y3], [c.x2, c.y2]].forEach(function () {});`, a no-op with an empty body left over from whatever drew the cart rails before — dead code, no player consequence. LIVE.wheel shadows its own `sp` parameter with the loop variable at 7149; it happens to be safe because anchorAt(sp, ...) runs at 7143 before the loop assigns 0, but it is one reordering away from breaking. The house eave transform `E(p) = [p[0]*eo, p[1]*eo]` scales about the ground origin rather than about the wall top, which lifts the whole eave ring a uniform 0.1*hgt = 7.8px and leaves a ~2.9px strip of the dark top diamond showing between the wall head and the roof — invisible, because the eave shadow band drawn at 6995-6996 covers it. The house ridge over-runs paintedBox by 8px on the square's largest houses, which finding 2 subsumes. The vat's hanging bar at 7718 is '#4a4238' against a transparent background and reads as nothing, so the four candles appear to hang from thin air; too small to bother with, and it is inside the shop where the counter is usually in front of it. All screenshots and probes are under .claude/nnqc/pnt2-*; nothing under comp/ was touched.

The roof does not. PAINT.house names its two planes `farPlane` and `nearPlane` and unconditionally paints far=unlit, near=lit — which only coincides with the light rule for one of the two ridge orientations.

Working the geometry: for !flip, rA = mid(eaveW, eaveN) and rB = mid(eaveE, eaveS), so the ridge runs along x at mid-y, and `nearPlane = [rA, rB, eaveS, eaveW]` is the plane whose eave corners are S=(b0+bw, b1+bh) and W=(b0, b1+bh), i.e. both at max y — the plane over the `sw` quad. Lighting it is correct. For flip, rA = mid(eaveN, eaveE) and rB = mid(eaveW, eaveS), so the ridge runs along y at mid-x, and `nearPlane = [rA, rB, eaveS, eaveE]` has corners S=(b0+bw, b1+bh) and E=(b0+bw, b1), both at max x — the plane over the `se` quad, the shadowed wall. So on every flip house the lit thatch/slate (`#4a3f28` / `#3a3945`) is directly above the dark plaster, and the unlit thatch (`#302819`) is above the lit plaster. PAINT.mill (7107-7108) uses the !flip layout only and is correct.

`flip = v % 2 === 1`, and running the game's own hash2/propVar over the twelve house footprints gives v = 4,0,5,4,1 (square), 4,3,1 (Grelling), 4,5,0,3 (a3sq): six of twelve are flip. CORNER MAPPING. spriteCtx (comp/ninth.js:6455-6462) sets sk=(bw-bh)*TILE_W/4, rrx=(bw+bh)*TILE_W/4, rry=(bw+bh)*TILE_H/4 and the four base corners. Substituting the footprint centre (X+bw/2, Y+bh/2) into isoXB/isoYB (58-59) and subtracting: (x0,y0)=(-sk,-rry) is world (X,Y)=N; (x1,y1)=(rrx,(bw-bh)*TILE_H/4) is world (X+bw,Y)=E; (x2,y2)=(sk,rry) is world (X+bw,Y+bh)=S; (x3,y3)=(-rrx,-(bw-bh)*TILE_H/4) is world (X,Y+bh)=W. Confirmed all four algebraically.

      CORRECTION: The finding is right in mechanism, code, lines and variant list. Two details in the write-up are wrong and should be fixed before it is filed:

1. "the unlit thatch (#302819) is above the lit plaster" is not what a player sees. On a flip house the unlit farPlane covers the -x half of the roof, over the NORTH-WEST face, which is a hidden wall; on screen it reads as the upper-left half of the roof. What actually sits directly above the lit SW plaster on a flip house is the GABLE triangle (6872: gable = [W.w, W.s, apex]), painted flat #3b3644 thatched / #37323f slate (luma 57.1), which is dimmer than the 73.3 wall under it. Correct statement: "on a flip house the bright roof plane sits over the dark wall and the flat gable sits over the lit wall, which is the reverse of every !flip house and of PAINT.mill."

2. The suggested fix's names are wrong for the flip branch. [rA, rB, eaveW, eaveN] is the plane over the NORTH-WEST face, not the south-west one, so calling it overSW just moves the lie. The behaviour it produces (lit = the -x plane) is correct. Name them for the world axis they slope toward, e.g. overMaxY/overMinY for !flip and overMinX/overMaxX for flip, or pass the wall quad's own lit flag through. Note also that the fix as written paints the near plane first and the far plane second in the flip case; the two quads do not overlap (they meet only along the ridge, and the ridge lines are drawn after both at 6986-6989), so this is harmless, but keeping far-then-near order is safer.

[N17] survived 2/2 (bug) sevVotes=['bug', 'bug']
  title: paintedBox() forgets the footprint, under-measures the paint by up to 91px, and the roof cutaway silently stops working
  where: comp/ninth.js:6510 paintedBox (`y0: -(c.hgt + over)`), read by comp/ninth.js:6669-6682 coversSomeone via comp/ninth.js:6493 `box: paintedBox(c)`
  what : The comment at 6502-6507 says measuring the paint is unnecessary because "`hgt + over` IS where the paint stops, because that is exactly the room the canvas was sized to give it". It is not. propSprite sizes the canvas as `ay = SPRITE_PAD + ceil(rry + hgt + over)` (6489) — it reserves rry as well, because the footprint's north corner sits at local y = -rry and anything standing there reaches -(rry + hgt). paintedBox drops the rry term. So for every prop the declared top is short by roughly rry - over, and contactShadow's outermost ring (6705, footprint x 1.18) makes it worse for flat props.

coversSomeone then does `if (sy > y1 || sy - a.h < y0) continue;` — a figure whose head is above the declared top of the paint is treated as visible and the prop does not thin. With y0 too low, figures who are entirely buried are read as sticking out.

Worked through for the chandler's east wall, `{ t: 'wall', b: [11.4, 0, 0.6, 9] }` (comp/ninth.js:5139): rry = 9.6*29/4 = 69.6, hgt 68, over 0, so paintedBox claims y0 = -68 while the wall's north top corner is painted at -137.6 (measured: -138). Footprint centre (11.7, 4.5), k = 16.2. Stand at (11.05, 2.4) — standable: the shelf [7.4,1.2,3.4,0.8] blocks only y <= 2.3 and the wall blocks only x >= 11.1. dx = -0.65, dy = -2.1, so a.k = 13.45 < 16.2 (behind), sy - myc = 14.5*(dx+dy) + 14.5 = -25.5, head = -69.5. -69.5 < -68 by 1.5px, so coversSomeone returns false. But the wall's `se` quad at that column (sx - mxc = 44.7) spans local y -81.6 to -13.6, so the player's whole -69.5..-25.5 is inside it. The failure region is x in [10.67, 11.09], y in (2.3, 13.545 - x): about 0.15 tile², in the corner of the shop you walk into to reach the shelf.

  title: The cart's front wheel is on its tailgate, fifteen pixels off the ground, because c.fw is used as a horizontal span
  where: comp/ninth.js:7442 (`var fw = c.fw`) and comp/ninth.js:7457-7464 (`var wx = fw * w[0]`), PAINT.cart
  what : `c.fw` (comp/ninth.js:6463) is `Math.hypot(sk + rrx, rry + (bw-bh)*TILE_H/4)` — the LENGTH of the sloped south-west iso edge, which is the exact quantity the bar() helper (6592-6600) was written to stop people using as a width. PAINT.cart is now the only reader of c.fw in the file (grep gives 6463, the bar() comment, 7442, 7458).

For the cart's footprint 2.2x1.2: sk = 14.5, rrx = 49.3, rry = 24.65, so fw = hypot(63.8, 31.9) = 71.33, while the horizontal span of the SW side is only 63.8 — fw is 11.8% too long, and it is also centred on the origin rather than on that side's midpoint at x = -17.4. The two wheels land at x = -0.3*71.33 = -21.4 (r 9) and x = +0.32*71.33 = +22.8 (r 8). The SW face runs from the west corner at x = -49.3 to the south corner at x = +14.5; the SE face (the cart's END) runs from +14.5 to +49.3. So the rear wheel is on the side, and the front wheel's entire 16px diameter, x 14.8..30.8, is on the tailgate.

It is also in the air. Both wheels are placed at `wy = ty + hgt*0.9 = -2.2`. At x = -21.4 the SW face's foot is at y = 6.7, so the rear wheel (r 9) just touches the ground. At x = +22.8 the SE face's foot is at y = 20.5, so the front wheel's lowest point is 5.8 — 14.7px clear of the ground.

A difference of 2/1/2. The rail runs from the west corner raised 12px to the south corner raised 12px, and crossing that line with the top diamond's NW edge shows it leaves the diamond only for x < -37.3, i.e. 12px of its 63.8px run. 81% of the cart's rail is painted in a colour two units from the surface underneath it and is invisible.

Both carts in the game are 2.2x1.2 (square [3.4,8.2], Grelling [9.6,7.4]) and share one sprite key, so this is every cart.

  title: The hedge floats fifteen pixels above its own contact shadow
  where: comp/ninth.js:7504, inside PAINT.hedge: `g.ellipse(hx + (rng()-0.5)*6, hy - hgt - lift + (rng()-0.5)*5, 9 + rng()*5, 6 + rng()*4, 0, 0, TAU)`
  what : `hx, hy` are `c.lx/c.ly` of a point on the footprint's long centre line, so they are on the GROUND. The blob's centre is then put at `hy - hgt - lift`, a full prop height (24) plus a per-pass lift (0, 3, 6) above that, with a vertical radius of only 6..10. A body drawn 24px above the ground with a 10px half-height has its lowest pixel 14px in the air; the correct centre for something `hgt` tall is `hy - hgt/2`.

ContactShadow (6695-6709) is drawn on the true footprint, so the shadow and the bush are separated by a clear band of floor. The blob also reaches to `hy - 24 - 6 - 10 = hy - 40`, 16px past its own declared height, which is where the measured over-run of 14px on the sprite comes from.

One instance in the game, `{ t: 'hedge', b: [8.4, 20.2, 1.6, 1.2] }` at comp/ninth.js:5245, standing beside the road where the fence ends.
  repro: Measured on the real baked sprite by .claude/nnqc/pnt2-measure.html (headless, --dump-dom):
  HEDGE 1.6x1.2  foliage on the centre column: local y -37 .. -15   (ground is 0, prop h is -24)
  lowest leaf is 15px clear of the ground it stands on

      Read PAINT.hedge at comp/ninth.js:7493-7514 verbatim.       CORRECTION: The mechanism is exactly what the code does and it reproduces, but two numbers are wrong and the severity is a notch high.

(a) hy is NOT constant along the run. ly(u,q) = (u+q)*TILE_H/2 - rry, so for the 1.6x1.2 instance hy = (1.6q + 0.6)*14.5 - 20.3 and sweeps -11.6 (back of the run) to +11.6 (front). The "14px in the air" is therefore not a single number: the lowest foliage pixel in the whole baked sprite is local y = -5 at local x = 21, where the footprint's near edge runs to y = +12.7, so the worst instance of the gap is ~18px, and the centre column's is 15px. Measured across the footprint: 73 of 81 columns carry foliage; on 40 of those the LOWEST foliage pixel is above that column's own far ground edge, i.e. the bush clears the diamond outright. Mean bare floor below the foliage is 28.1px per column against a mean footprint column height of only rry = 20.3px, so on average the whole shadow diamond is exposed with ~8px of air on top. "Separated by a clear band of floor" is therefore true for most columns but not all: on the back columns the foliage does clip the diamond's north corner by about 6px.

(b) "reaches hy - 40, 16px past its own declared height" is muddled arithmetic. hgt + over = 24 + 16 = 40 IS the declared top: paintedBox (6508-6511) returns y0 = -(hgt + over) = -40. So hy-40 at hy=0 is exactly at the declared box, not past it. The real over-run comes from hy = -11.6 at the back of the run plus the +/-2.5 jitter and the 6..10 radius: the topmost opaque pixel in the baked sprite is local y = -53, which is 13px (not 14, not 16) above paintedBox's y0. Nothing is clipped, because SPRITE_PAD = 30 covers it, but coversSomeone (6668-6681) reads that box.

(c) Severity. There is exactly one instance, in `road`, which is night:1. The contact shadow is three stacked bands compositing to alpha ~140 and it sits under a 0.5 dark-blue veil plus the vignette, so at hz=1 the thing reads as a flat green pancake in the dark rather than as an object visibly hovering over its own shadow. Real geometry defect, single decorative prop, comparable in reach to _recovered.txt [4] (five sill lights glowing on the void), which was filed polish.

The prescribed fix is sound in direction. Note that hy - hgt/2 - lift still puts the topmost pixel at about -42 for the back of the run, so `over: 16` stays marginally understated either way.

3. Reach. The one instance is in `road`, night:1, with no lamp source nearer than 10 tiles (the last lamp is at 8.2,30.4) so the only light on it is the player's own. That is why I make this polish rather than bug: round one graded the same genre of defect as bug for PAINT.fence ([20]), but that one is 9 instances across 5 places including lit ones; this is 1 instance in the darkest outdoor place in the game.

Everything else checks out and I could not knock it down. hy is genuinely the ground point (ly(bw/2,bh/2) = 0), the blob centre is genuinely put a full hgt=24 plus lift above it with a vertical radius of only 6..10, and PAINT.tree at 7181 shows the convention the hedge copied without bringing the trunk: the tree draws roots at y=0 and stacked trunk slabs from 0 to -hgt before putting the canopy at ty=-hgt, so its canopy at -hgt is correct. The hedge has nothing between the ground and the leaves. It is harmless today because RT.hide only ever holds the player and the hedge is 24 tall.

The suggested fix is right in shape: hy - hgt/2 - lift at 7504 gives blobs spanning hy-24.5 .. hy+0.5, which grounds the mass and keeps its top near the declared height, and 7512 must move with it.

  title: The dither on every round body is thrown at a bounding rectangle rather than the object, so twenty-four props carry a halo of loose speckle
  where: comp/ninth.js:7471-7473 (PAINT.stone / PAINT.cairn `bb`), comp/ninth.js:7428-7430 (PAINT.sack `bb`), comp/ninth.js:7230-7232 (PAINT.tree `bb`), and comp/ninth.js:7490 (the cairn's stacked-stone dither)
  what : dither() (6549-6577) walks whatever quad it is handed. Every flat-faced painter hands it a real face — `dither(g, f.sw, ...)`, `dither(g, rub, ...)`, `dither(g, blanket, ...)` — but the four round-bodied painters build an axis-aligned rectangle `bb` around the object and dither that instead. Where the ellipse does not fill the rectangle, the 2px cells land on transparent pixels and are blitted straight onto the ground.

For stone 1x1 the outer roundBody ellipse (6729) is rx 33.4, ry 30.4 against a bb of 69.6 x 60.8, so about a quarter of the rectangle is empty; for the tree the bb (7230) is a plain rect 1.6*rrx by 2*rry around a canopy made of nine scattered clumps, so much more of it is empty.

Count of affected props: tree 8 (lane 2, mark 1, road 3, hollow 2), stone 8 (lane 1, road 2, hollow 5), cairn 3 (road 2, hollow 1), sack 5 (mill 3, loft 2) — 24. The cairn is the worst on screen because it dithers twice, once round the base stone and once round the stacked one at 7490.
  repro: Measured on the real sprites by .claude/nnqc/pnt2-measure.html, counting painted pixels inside the bb rectangle that fall outside the body ellipse:
  stone 1x1    inside 2143, outside 295 (12.1%), well clear of it 28
  cairn 1.2x1.2 inside 3203, outside 375 (10.5%), well clear 49
  sack 1.2x1   inside 1742, outside 181 (9.4%), well clear 24
  tree 1.4x1.4 inside 1533, outside 450 (22.7%), well clear 121
Visible as a grey speckle cloud round the cairn in .claude/nnqc/pnt2-p-veg.png. In play, a row scan of .claude/nnqc/pnt2-tree2.png (lane, hz=4) finds green cells at image x 688 on rows y 68 and 76 while the canopy proper starts at x 816 on the row below — detached leaf cells about 16 canvas px out in open air.
  fix  : dither() already handles any convex quad, so give it the shape instead of the box. Cheapest correct version: clip to the body before dithering — `g.save(); g.beginPath(); g.ellipse(0, -hgt/2, rrx*0.96, rry + hgt/2, 0, 0, TAU); g.clip(); dither(...); g.restore();` in PAINT.stone/cairn and PAINT.sack, and clip to the union of the clump ellipses in PAINT.tree. The dither's own comment about dropping the clip was about scanline cost inside a parallelogram, not about silhouettes.
   --repro-- refuted=False conf=high

Mechanism check. dither() walks the quad's own scanlines and fills with one path; there is no clip, and the comment at 6551-6556 explains the clip was dropped because "every face here is a parallelogram", i.e. the quad IS the silhouette. The four cited painters break that premise by handing it an axis-aligned box. Verified there is no guard downstream: paintProp (6688-6691) applies no clip or composite mode, propSprite (6485-6503) paints into a fresh transparent canvas, and drawProp (6621-6660) blits it with a plain drawImage (globalAlpha only, for the roof cut-through). So anything painted outside the body is composited straight onto the floor.

[N22] survived 2/2 (polish) sevVotes=['polish', 'polish']
  title: The mill wheel is the one moving thing in Wick and the one thing not made of pixels, bolted to the only completely untextured prop in the game
  where: comp/ninth.js:7132-7141 PAINT.wheel and comp/ninth.js:7142-7179 LIVE.wheel
  what : Two halves of the same prop, both weak.

The sprite: PAINT.wheel is `body(g, c, [...])` plus five px() rectangles (the axle post and its bearing block) and nothing else. No boarding, no dither, no seams, no course lines — it is the only prop with a bespoke painter that leaves both faces and the top of body() exactly as body() left them. At b [10.8, 1.6, 1.2, 3.2] (comp/ninth.js:5175) that is a 128x70px solid the size of a small outbuilding standing beside the mill. Every other painter that starts from body() puts something on it: PAINT.beam adds seams and a lit arris, PAINT.crate battens and a diagonal, PAINT.post mortises and lichen, PAINT.cart plank seams, PAINT.markstone chisel work.

The wheel itself: because it turns it cannot live in the sprite, so LIVE.wheel redraws it every frame — and it draws it entirely with raw anti-aliased context calls. `cx.arc(mxc, cy, wr, ...)` twice for the rim at lineWidth 6 and 3 (7146, 7148), twelve `cx.stroke()` spokes and twelve paddle strokes (7152, 7159, 7169), and two `cx.arc` fills for the hub (7177-7178). None of it goes through px/poly/line, all of which round to integers. contactShadow's own comment (6702) calls a soft falloff "the one thing on screen that is not made of pixels"; this is a 64px-diameter object silhouette with the same property, and it is the only thing in the world that moves, so the eye goes to it.

For the record, going through all ~60 raw ctx sites in the painter block: six are defensible glows and soft matter (the sill lamp 6961-6963, the chimney smoke 7050, the mill's flour dust 7129, the nine footlights 7564-7567, the lamp 7612-7616, the hearth 7778-7782). The rest are object silhouettes or texture blobs, and this one is the largest, the sharpest-edged and the only animated one.
  repro: Sprite alone, no LIVE layer, with the footprint drawn on: .claude/nnqc/pnt2-p-wheel.png (.claude/nnqc/pnt2-portraits.html?dev=ninth&p=wheel:1.2x3.2,stagewip:4.6x2.6,markstone:1.4x1.6&s=3) — a featureless brown box with one small post on its face. 1. TITLE IS WRONG: "the only completely untextured prop in the game". `table` (5122, bernhouse, 2.6x1.6) and `counter` (5140, chandler, 4.8x1.2) have no PAINT entry at all and fall through PAINT._ (6744-6748) to a bare body() with zero decoration. 3. "it is the only thing in the world that moves" - false. NPCs walk (stepNpcs), the player walks, and LIVE.house/mill/foot/lamp/hearth all animate, as do the wheel's own drips (7171-7173). The tree canopy (7223-7228) is 27 raw ellipse fills roughly 66x52 across, with quadraticCurveTo limb strokes at lineWidth 3 (7203-7206), and there are eight trees. Raw anti-aliased curves are an established idiom in this painter block for round things, not a one-off lapse. What is actually distinctive about the wheel is that it is a thin high-contrast stroke with two soft edges per line, and that it turns, so the eye tracks it.

5. "None of it goes through px/poly/line, all of which round to integers" - 7162-7163 (the mended plate's two nails) and 7173 (the drip) do use Math.round + fillRect. Three of the calls are integer-aligned; the rim, spokes and paddles are not.

6. Citation slip: the wheel prop entry is comp/ninth.js:5177, not 5175.

Trimmed statement: the mill wheel's frame is the only bespoke-painted body() in the game left with both faces and its top bare, a 128x134px flat brown box; and the turning wheel bolted to it is drawn with raw anti-aliased cx.arc/stroke calls, giving a 64px moving object a two-to-three-pixel soft edge in a scene where the fence beside it is hard-edged. Table and counter are equally bare but have no painter at all, and raw ellipse silhouettes are common elsewhere (roundBody, cylBody, the tree canopy); the wheel stands out because it is a thin stroke and because it moves.

[N23] REFUTED (polish) sevVotes=[]
  title: The contact shadow of the five longest props is cut square by SPRITE_PAD
  where: comp/ninth.js:6435 `SPRITE_PAD = 30` and comp/ninth.js:6485-6486 (canvas sizing) against comp/ninth.js:6705 in contactShadow (the outermost ring at 1.18x the footprint)
  what : propSprite sizes the canvas as `w = ceil(rrx*2) + SPRITE_PAD*2` with the origin at `ax = SPRITE_PAD + ceil(rrx)`, so there are exactly 30px of room either side of the footprint's west and east corners. contactShadow scales the footprint quad by 1.18, which needs 0.18*rrx px of room. The two cross over at rrx = 166.7, i.e. bw + bh = 11.5 — and five sprites in the game are past it.

  curtain 13x1.4  rrx 208.8, needs 37.6, has 30 — 7.6px of shadow lost each end
  foot 13x0.5     rrx 195.8, needs 35.2 — 5.2px lost
  wall 12x0.6     rrx 182.7, needs 32.9 — 2.9px lost
  wall 11x0.6     rrx 168.2, needs 30.3 — 0.3px lost
  stagewip 8x3.2  rrx 162.4, needs 29.2 — touches the last column

The brief's own premise was that the canvas has slack because it includes PAD and rry. It does on the vertical axis; it does not on the horizontal one, and this is the first thing to run out of it. The consequence is faint — the outer ring is rgba(5,4,9,.15) — but it is a straight vertical cut across a shadow that is otherwise a diamond, at the west and east tips of the stage curtain, the footlight strip, both interior north walls and the Act 3 stage.
  repro: Measured, not derived: .claude/nnqc/pnt2-sprites.html reports the alpha bounding box of every baked sprite and flags any that touch a canvas edge. It prints LEFT RIGHT for curtain 13x1.4, foot 13x0.5 and wall 12x0.6, and RIGHT for wall 11x0.6 and stagewip 8x3.2; every other row is clear. Visible as a hard-edged grey tab beyond the curtain's south corner in .claude/nnqc/pnt2-p-long.png.
  fix  : Size the pad off the shadow rather than off a constant: `var pad = SPRITE_PAD + Math.ceil(rrx * 0.18)` for the width term in propSprite, and use it for ax as well. Costs 4% on the widest sprite. Alternatively shrink the outer shadow ring from 1.18 to 1.0 + 30/rrx for long props, but that changes how nine fences look.

  what : Two things wrong in one expression. Somebody meant to pick one of two corners and always gets the same one.

Second, both surviving corners are wall-END values used at the window's position. The quad is 22px wide and centred on the lamp at `lx2`, but its bottom-right corner is pinned to the EAST corner's ground y (c.y1) and its bottom-left to the SOUTH corner's ground y (c.y2). REPRO 2 — wrote my 
      CORRECTION: Real, and if anything understated on the geometry while overstated on visibility. Four corrections:

1. The dead branch is not a coin-flip that always lands the same way, it is a min() that can never take its other arm. `sw[2][1]` and `se[3][1]` are literally the same point: `sw = [.., .., [c.x2, c.y2], ..]` and `se = [.., .., .., [c.x2, c.y2]]` (comp/ninth.js:6876-6877). So the expression reduces to `min(se[3][1], se[2][1])`, and since rry = (bw+bh)*TILE_H/4 > (bw-bh)*TILE_H/4 for any bh > 0, it is always `se[2][1]`. Checked against every house in the game: all 12 static `{ t: 'house', b: [...] }` entries (comp/ninth.js:5092-5093, 5206, 5283-5284) take the true branch, and no house is created anywhere else, so `se[3][1]` in that slot is unreachable in the shipped build.

2. "raked at about 66 degrees to the surface it is supposed to be running down" mixes two frames. 66 degrees (atan(49.3/22) = 65.9) is the quad's lower edge measured from horizontal. The wall foot is at 26.6 degrees. The error relative to the wall is about 39 degrees.

3. The 4x3.4 house is not the worst case. Measured on the real baked sprites: square `{ t: 'house', b: [0,0,4,3.4] }` (v=4, lampU 0.30) has 37-41 warm pixels below the SE wall foot, worst overhang 11.0px at local x 34 — the finding's number, reproduced exactly. But square `{ t: 'house', b: [13.4,11,3.6,4] }` (v=1, lampU 0.60) has 195-230 warm pixels below the foot, worst overhang 29.8px at local x 60, with the quad's south corner sitting 33.1px below the wall. lampU 0.60 houses are all worse: the quad's fixed +-11px half-width spans a bigger fraction of the remaining wall.

4. The visible cost is smaller than "amber paint on the cobbles" suggests. A/B capture of the same square frame with and without line 6969 (patched copy of ninth.js, not the original) shows the entire quad, wall part included, changes the finished picture by at most 8 levels out of 255, median 1. The night wash `rgba(6,5,14,.5)`, the lamp's own 26px radial glow drawn in `lighter` right on top of it, and the contact shadow underneath all flatten it. What a player can actually see is a faint hard-edged pale tail hanging below the wall foot onto the ground, visible at 4x magnification, invisible at 1x. Polish is the right severity; this is not something anyone would file.

######## LENS: THE CUTAWAY AND THE PAINTER SORT
NOTES: HARNESS WARNING, and it invalidates any capture taken the documented way: the dev server on port 8492 is NOT serving this worktree. Its /comp/ninth.js is 14012 lines (md5 805c4891...) against this worktree's 8279 (md5 6868ebe2...); the diff is a whole punch/zoom TUNE block and a screenToWorld that undoes a zoom punch. Everything below was captured against a python http.server rooted at this worktree on 127.0.0.1:8497, byte-verified against comp/ninth.js first. Substitute 8497 for 8492 in every URL here.
Method: .claude/nnqc/cut2-mask.html hooks CanvasRenderingContext2D.drawImage, catches every prop sprite the real game blits in all 13 places, reads its alpha back, and compares the true silhouette against paintedBox()/coversSomeone(). Every coverage number below is measured pixels at alpha > 200 (so the translucent contact shadow is never counted as cover), not a bounding-box estimate. Node side: .claude/nnqc/cut2-spurious.js. Raw output: .claude/nnqc/cut2h.txt (and cut2a/c/d/f.txt for the earlier passes). Nothing under comp/ was touched.
o._ci is clean. drawProp has exactly one call site (comp/ninth.js:3887, inside the ents closure), o._ci is assigned at 3886 in the same forEach that builds those closures, and gotoPlace runs RESETS at comp/ninth.js:6123 which includes the onPlaceChange at 6665 clearing RT.world.cut. It cannot be read before it is written and it cannot go stale across a place change.
The fade is frame-rate independent and correct. 1 - pow(0.004, dt) has a time constant of 1/ln(250) = 0.181s; the per-frame step differs at 30fps (0.168) and 144fps (0.0376) but the elapsed-time curve is identical. RT.rdt is assigned at comp/ninth.js:3881, before the ents array is walked, so it is never undefined on the first frame, and the `|| 1/60` at 6649 covers dt === 0. draw() is called with the REAL dt (frame(): `draw(real)` at 3811), so the cutaway does not slow down during a recital while everything else does; that is a deliberate-looking split and I would leave it.
Sort keys are unique and the order is deterministic. No two props in any place share a k (checked to six decimals), and no NPC shares a k with a prop. On a tie the sort is stable (V8 TimSort, ES2019) and the push order is foes, then NPCs, then props, then the player, so the player always wins a tie, which is the safe direction.
T_CUT = 0.56 genuinely works when it fires. .claude/nnqc/cut2-sq-through-roof.png (square, nat=12.5,10.2, hz=3) has the player standing under the south-east house with the roof thinned: the cobbles read through the thatch and the figure is unambiguous. Same in .claude/nnqc/cut2-chand-ctrl.png and cut2-sq-house-ctrl.png.
The off-camera cull at comp/ninth.js:6625-6626 is safe for every prop in the game: the tallest sprite is the mill at rry+hgt+over = 250.2px against a 260px top margin, and the eaves' 10% overhang stays inside the 80px side margin.
The a3sq foot strip and the stage's 13-wide foot strip can never trigger the cutaway at all (rry + hgt + over vs the 44px figure leaves an empty interval), which is right for the a3sq one and wrong-but-tiny for the stage one (30% of the player's boots behind it, 0.5 tiles of ground).
paintedBox's x half-width of rrx*1.15 is fine: at alpha > 200 only 2 of 96 prop instances paint wider than it, both by 1px. The lateral margin is not the problem; the top is.
Foes are excluded from RT.hide as well as NPCs, and that is only load-bearing in a3sq (finding 3). Elsewhere the ground where a 28x32 foe would be more than half hidden is 1.8% of the lane, 2.4% of the loft, 2.8% of the mark and the road, 3.8% of the hollow, 8.8% of the mill — small, and foes chase you, so they do not sit there.

In coversSomeone, with du = (a.x - a.y) - (cxw - cyw) and dv = (a.x + a.y) - (cxw + cyw) in tiles, the footprint's dv range at that column is [2*max(-bw/2, du - bh/2) - du, 2*min(bw/2, du + bh/2) - du], empty when |du| > (bw+bh)/2. Then the prop covers the actor iff that range is non-empty, dv is below its low end (the actor is on the far side), and the actor's screen span [sy - a.h, sy] overlaps [dvLo*14.5 - (hgt+over), dvHi*14.5].       CORRECTION: Real but overstated, and the wrong prop is in the headline. What is true: paintedBox is a loose AABB and coversSomeone fires where none of the prop's paint is on the player. At stage (3,3) the whole 13-tile backdrop drops to T_CUT while the player's crown pixel is byte-identical to the no-cut control (255,251,119) — nothing of the curtain is over them. What is false: "it can never legitimately be in your way at all" and "every one of those 9.3 tiles is spurious". The curtain is drawn after the player for every px+py < 7.2 (ents sort on k = 7.2, ninth.js:3887-3891) and its drape reaches down to the y=1.4 edge, so with actors drawn at isoY + TILE_H/2 it lands on the player's head for py < 2.417 — 20.8px of the 44px figure at the closest walkable approach, py = 1.7. So 3.2 of the 9.1 firing tiles are a genuine cover the cut is load-bearing for, and 5.9 tiles (6.4% of the prologue floor, not 10.2%) are the spurious part. The finding's own cut2-spurious.js mis-scores this because its spurious test is "the prop's ground is behind you" (vm <= v), which ignores both the prop's height and the half tile coversSomeone itself adds; the right threshold is v < vmax(u) + 2.03. Same correction applies to its list: only bernhouse wall[0,0,11,0.6] is wholly spurious; the two 0.6x9 side walls are ~69% real.       CORRECTION: The firing region is right; the reason given for it is wrong, and about 40% of it is not spurious.

What is confirmed. paintedBox (comp/ninth.js:6508-6511) and coversSomeone (comp/ninth.js:6669-6681) are as quoted. For the stage curtain (comp/ninth.js:5082, b [0,0,13,1.4]) rrx=208.8, rry=104.4, x0/x1 = ±240.12, y1 = 108.4, and with PROP.curtain h:120 over:16 (comp/ninth.js:6391) y0 = -136. k = 7.2. Integrating over the walkable set gives 9.04 tiles of an 88.6-tile floor, 10.2%. Measured live: RT.world.cut[0] = 0.56 at (2,1.8), (3,3) and (5,1.8), and 1 at (6.5,1.8) and (8,3). The visible symptom is real: .claude/nnqc/sk-fix-B.png (nat=3,3) against .claude/nnqc/sk-fix-C.png (nat=8,3), same clamped camera, same crop, shows the whole backdrop washed out in one and saturated in the other.

What is wrong. (1) "It can never occlude anybody" is false. The finding treats the curtain as a flat ground band; PAINT.curtain (comp/ninth.js:7515-7538) paints one quad from (c.x3, c.y3 - hgt) to (c.x2, c.y2), i.e. a 120px-tall vertical plane standing on the footprint's SOUTH edge at world y = 1.4, plus 5px of pole. For any south-facing plane at world y = Y, a player at (px,py) has their feet 14.5*(2*(py-Y)+1) px below the plane's bottom edge at their own screen column, so at the collision limit py = Y+0.3 that is 23.2px and the plane covers the top 20.8 of the player's 44 rows (RT.hide h:44, comp/ninth.js:3882; drawActor draws feet at isoY+TILE_H/2, comp/ninth.js:2111, which is exactly what coversSomeone models). The curtain draws after the player whenever px+py < 7.2, so it really does paint over them. Measured, not argued: a canvas colour scan 24px east of the player (probe .claude/nnqc/skep-cut-probe.html) puts the curtain/floor boundary at feet-14 for nat=2,1.8 (predicted -14.1) and at feet-49 for nat=3,3 (predicted -48.9). At (2,1.8) the curtain covers 30 of the player's 44 rows. The cutaway is doing its job there.
(2) The "40px thick band of paint" figure is wrong by the whole height of the prop. The paint is a 377x120 parallelogram, about 39% of the 480x244 box, and on every column it exists on it spans most of the box's vertical range. y1 = rry + 4 is not an inflation either: rry is exactly the footprint's own south corner, and a box that stopped short of it would clip the prop. The real cause of the (3,3) false positive is that one axis-aligned rectangle is the union over all columns of a prop 14.4 tiles long, so the rows near the diamond's west corner get the y-range that belongs to the east corner. That is a bounding-box-versus-paint problem, but a different one from the one described.
(3) "Nine tiles clear of it" is the area figure read as a distance. At (3,3) the player is 1.6 tiles south of the footprint and the curtain's bottom edge clears the top of their head by about 12px at their own column, less than one tile of screen height.
(4) The corrected split: of the 9.04 firing tiles, the curtain genuinely paints over the player wherever py < 2.42 (2.59 once you allow the sprite's ~10px half-width), which is 3.4 to 4.1 tiles. The spurious remainder is about 5.0 to 5.6 tiles, 6% of the prologue floor, not 100% of the firing region.
(5) The "all of it spurious" list does not hold either, and one entry on it fails in the opposite direction. bernhouse wall [0,0,11,0.6] at nat=1,0.9 reads cut = 1 for every prop: the wall does not fire at all, because coversSomeone's second clause (`sy - a.h < y0` -> continue) requires the actor's whole 44px span to be INSIDE the box rather than overlapping it, and the head at -86.05 is above y0 = -68. The same pixel scan shows the wall's bottom edge at feet-11 there, so the wall paints over 33 of the player's 44 rows at full opacity. That is a false negative in the same function, which is the sibling finding, and it means the bernhouse and chandler walls are mis-described here.

Severity. This is a courtesy overlay over-firing on roughly 5 tiles of one 13x11 prologue floor. Nothing is hidden, nothing is unreachable, no input is affected; the backdrop goes pale for a few steps in the first scene. Polish, not bug. The proposed per-column fix is still the right shape, but it has to be sold on the residual 5 tiles, not on a curtain that "can never be in your way".

(1) Sprite cost. propSprite (comp/ninth.js:6477) sizes the canvas w = ceil((bw+bh)*29)+60, h = ceil((bw+bh)*14.5 + hgt + over)+60. And the three thirds do not cost "0.2MB together": the sprite key is t|bw|bh|v and propVar returns 0 because PROP.wall has no `vars`, so three identical [0.6,3] segments share ONE cache entry of 165x181x4 = 119,460 bytes = 0.114 MB. The split is a 0.25 MB saving, better than the finding claims, not worse.

(2) The proposed split does not remove the case entirely. Segment keys become 12.2 / 15.2 / 18.2; at the room's extreme south-east (10.1, 8.5) the player key is 18.6, still ahead of the third segment's 18.2, and that column's wall runs v 19.2 to 19.6 so it still fully encloses the figure. The residual region is about 0.05 tiles squared instead of 3.2, so "removes both 100% cases" should read "shrinks them by ~98%".

(3) "everyone at x+y > 15.2 draws after it, including the whole south-east quadrant" is loose. Sorting after the wall only produces a visible defect where the wall's paint also overlaps the 32x40 figure rect, which needs 29*px > 219.1 + 40, i.e. px > 8.93 as well as px+py > 15.2. The finding's own worked example and its 3.2-tile measurement already use exactly this, so only the headline sentence overstates.

  title: The guard that is supposed to stop small props triggering the cutaway is unreachable; what actually keeps a sack from ghosting is an arithmetic coincidence nobody wrote down
  where: comp/ninth.js:6672, `if (x1 - x0 < 24 || y1 - y0 < 24) return false;   // low things never hide anybody`
  what : x1 - x0 is 2 * 1.15 * rrx = 33.35 * (bw + bh), so it is under 24 only for a prop smaller than 0.72 tiles on both axes together — the smallest in the game is the lamp at 0.5 x 0.5, which gives 33.35. y1 - y0 is hgt + over + rry + 4, and the smallest in the game is the 1.2 x 1 sack at 33.95. The guard has never returned false and cannot with any prop in PROP.
What actually stops a sack or a stone from thinning is that the two remaining clauses become mutually exclusive: coverage needs sy <= myc + rry + 4 and sy >= myc - (hgt + over) + 44 at once, which is only satisfiable when rry + hgt + over >= 40. sack 1.2x1 gives 29.95, stone 1x1 gives 34.5, crate 1.2x1.2 gives 37.4 — all impossible, so those props are silent by luck. crate 1.6x1.2 gives 40.3, a firing window 0.3px wide; the road and hollow cairns give windows of 3.4 and 6.3px. Nobody has noticed because the windows are sub-pixel, but the guard is not what is holding them shut, and the fix for finding 1 (raising the box top by rry, or changing containment to overlap) removes that coincidence entirely: with either fix in place every stone, sack, crate and cairn in the game becomes eligible to fade to 56% when you walk behind it. Seven lamps are already eligible today.
  repro: Arithmetic over the PROP table at comp/ninth.js:6388-6417: min over all 96 authored instances of 33.35*(bw+bh) is 33.35 and of hgt+over+rry+4 is 33.95, both above 24. Cross-checked against the live sweep in .claude/nnqc/cut2h.txt, where no sack, stone or crate appears in any firing list for any place.
  fix  : Make the guard say what it means, in terms of paint rather than box: `if (c.hgt + (c.d.over || 0) < 30) return false;` — a thing under 30px tall never hides a 40px figure by enough to be worth dissolving a prop for. That keeps sacks, stones, crates, beams and the stage's footlight strip solid after finding 1 is fixed, and leaves lamps, posts, cairns and everything above them eligible.

[N30] survived 2/2 (bug) sevVotes=['bug', 'polish']
  title: Both road fences and the lane's east fence can be walked straight past on the outermost walkable line, so "past the fence" is a thing you do by accident
  where: comp/ninth.js:5239 (PLACES.road fences [0.8,23.6,0.5,8.4] and [9.7,23.6,0.5,8.4]), comp/ninth.js:5156 (PLACES.lane [11.6,3,0.5,3.2] and [11.6,10.2,0.5,2.8]); mechanism in blocked() comp/ninth.js:6044-6050 and moveActor comp/ninth.js:6052-6058
  what : moveActor clamps to `nx = clamp(nx, 0.5, W - 0.5)` and blocked() is a strict AABB: `x + r > b[0] && x - r < b[0] + b[2]`. A fence therefore only seals the edge of the world if b[0] - 0.3 < 0.5 on the west side, or b[0] + b[2] + 0.3 > W - 0.5 on the east. Worked per fence at r = 0.3: road is 11 wide, so the brink lines are x = 0.5 and x = 10.5. West fence: blocked requires 0.5 < x < 1.6, and x = 0.5 fails `x + r > b[0]` by exactly zero (0.8 > 0.8 is false). East fence: blocked requires 9.4 < x < 10.5, and x = 10.5 fails `x - r < b[0] + b[2]` by exactly zero (10.2 < 10.2 is false). Lane is 13 wide, brink x = 12.5; its east pair blocks 11.3 < x < 12.4, so the strip (12.4, 12.5] is free by 0.1 tiles. And clamp() puts you on exactly that line the instant you push into the edge, so it is not a pixel-hunt: hold the key into the wall.

The mill's and the mark's fences do hold (mill west b[0]=0.6 in a 15-wide place, mill east ends at 14.4 > 14.2, mark west b[0]=0.6), so this is not the whole fence system, it is the two places whose fences were authored flush to the boundary.

What it costs: on the road the fence IS the story. PLACES.road look 'Where the fence ends' (comp/ninth.js:5249) reads "In the song he walks out past the fence. He is the only one in it who is ever said to have done that. You are standing past it now. It took nine steps." A player who walked north hugging either verge was outside the fence for its whole 8.4-tile length before that line ever fired. In the lane it means the east fence is decoration: you can cross the boundary line anywhere and reach the Grelling exit without ever using the gap it was cut for.

      Re-derived from source and reproduced independently.

CODE, as read: blocked() at comp/ninth.js:6044-6050 is `if (x + r > b[0] && x - r < b[0] + b[2] && y + r > b[1] && y - r < b[1] + b[3]) return true;` — strictly-open AABB on all four sides. moveActor at 6052-6058 is `nx = clamp(nx, 0.5, W - 0.5); ny = clamp(ny, 0.5, H - 0.5);` with r defaulting to 0.3, and clamp is the plain `v < a ? a : v > b ? b : v` at line 36. Both stepPlayer call sites (1743, 1747) pass no r, so r = 0.3. So a boundary-hugging player sits at exactly x = 0.5 or x = W - 0.5 and the strict `>` / `<` decide the case.

DATA, at the cited lines: road (w:11) fences at comp/ninth.js:5239 are [0.8,23.6,0.5,8.4] and [9.7,23.6,0.5,8.4]; lane (w:13) fences at 5156 include [11.6,3,0.5,3.2] and [11.6,10.2,0.5,2.8]. Mill 5179 [0.6,...]/[13.9,...] in w:15, mark 5221 [0.6,...] in w:13 — the finding's "these hold" list is right.

ARITHMETIC, including the floating-point ties, which is where I expected this to die: 0.5 + 0.3 evaluates to 0.80000000000000004441, and the literal 0.8 is the same double, so `x + r > b[0]` is false by exact equality. 10.5 - 0.3 and 9.7 + 0.5 both evaluate to 10.199999999999999289, so `x - r < b[0] + b[2]` is false by exact equality.       CORRECTION: Real, and the mechanism is exactly as described. Four corrections, none of which save it:

1. The repro transcript is garbled and undersells how easy this is. "start (0.5,33), hold w+d ... (0.500,33.500)" cannot happen: w+d normalises to (0,-1), due north, so y falls. More to the point, you cannot get to x=0.5 by holding 'w' from mid-road — from (6,33) holding 'w' wedges you at (1.6277, 23.3067), pinned between the west post [0.9,22.4,0.6,0.6] and the fence, and holding 'd' wedges you at (9.3823, 23.3067) on the east post. The route that works is the one players actually take in a corridor: hold screen-left ('a') until you stop at (0.5, 33.5), then hold screen-up ('w'). That single second key then walks x=0.5 from y 33.5 to y 0.5 without ever stopping. East is hold 's' to (10.5, 33.5), then w+d.

2. "before that line ever fired" is wrong in the player's favour. nearestInteract (comp/ninth.js:6229-6236) uses a 1.9-tile radius and the look sits at (5.2, 22.2); a verge walker is never closer than 4.7 tiles (west) or 5.3 (east). "Where the fence ends" does not fire late — it never fires at all, so the player walks the length of the fence, past it, and out of the place having never been told the fence exists.

3. The finding stops one prop short. The road's two posts are flush the same way: [0.9,22.4,0.6,0.6] blocks only 0.6 < x < 1.8 and [9.6,22.4,0.6,0.6] only 9.3 < x < 10.5, so at x = 0.5 and x = 10.5 nothing on either verge is solid for the whole 34-tile length of the road, posts included. The fix must move all four boxes, not two.

4. The lane wording overreaches slightly. Both east fences leak, not just the upper one (confirmed both directions from the NE and SE corners), but either slide still deposits you in the village exit band (x 11.85..12.75, y 6.5..9.5), so no unintended route opens — the lane defect is that you walk through a fence, not that you get somewhere you should not. Approaching either lane fence from the west blocks correctly (jams at x = 11.2787). So this is an edge-strip leak, not a dead fence.
