1) Face naming and the wall's extent. "the wall's visible west face is the parallelogram (832.6,213.3)(832.6,281.3)(571.6,411.8)(571.6,343.8)" is the x=10.4 plane, which faces AWAY from the camera and is never drawn (body() at comp/ninth.js:6714 paints top + sw + se only). The correct statement is that the occluding edge is the north-west edge of the wall's TOP face, which projects along y = 629.6 - 0.5*sx and happens to coincide with the top of that back plane — so the finding's upper bound (279.6 at sx=700) is right by accident. Its lower bound is not: the real silhouette is the hull of the ground diamond and the ground diamond raised 68, whose lower edge is y = 715 - 0.5*sx, i.e. 365 at sx=700, not 347.6. The wall covers more than the finding says, so the bed conclusion is unchanged (the bed's lowest pixel at sx=700 is 336).

Cleaner way to state the whole mechanism: a wall of h=68 buys 68/29 = 2.345 tiles of x-occlusion, so bernhouse's east wall hides every ground pixel with x > 8.055 and the chandler's hides every ground pixel with x > 9.055. A pixel h above ground is hidden where 29x - h > 233.6 (bernhouse) or > 262.6 (chandler).

2) The chandler lamp is overstated. Not "the bottom 38% of its 75.6px". The lamp's silhouette at its own centre column runs from myc - (hgt+over) = myc-64 to myc + rry = myc+17.4, i.e. 71.3px (mxc,myc = 650.9, 336.15 with cam (43.5,-39)). 3) Worth adding, because it cuts against the fix as written: the strip east of 9.055 is not uniformly hidden. The chandler's barrel [9.8,6.6,1.2,1.2] sits deeper in it than the lamp (fully inside the wall's silhouette by the geometry above) but has k = 17.6 against the wall's 16.2, so the painter sort draws it OVER the wall and it shows in full. Visible in skep-chand-whole.png and skep-chand-lampzoom.png. So what is hidden and what is not is decided by the crude centre-k of a 9-tile-long prop, not by depth — the same authoring strip produces both a fully hidden lamp and a barrel floating in front of a wall it is behind.

The proposed fix is sound. Dropping the east wall does not break collision: moveActor clamps to 0.5..W-0.5 independently of props.

      CORRECTION: The core claim stands: the look at comp/ninth.js:5250 calls the cairn "on the west side of the road" and the cairn at comp/ninth.js:5243 is on the east side. Two supporting claims are wrong and should be struck.

(1) "it also straddles the eastern cart rut, which round one measured at u = 6.7" is false. GROUND.road (comp/ninth.js:1132-1152) draws the ruts as `var fence = f.gh * 0.66;` then `for (var v2 = f.gh; v2 > fence - 0.6; v2 -= 0.12)`. With gh = 34 that is fence = 22.44, so the ruts exist only for y from 34 down to about 21.84. The cairn is at y 9.4..10.6, twelve tiles north of where the ruts stop. The function's own header comment says "packed dirt, and the ruts stop before the end". There is no rut anywhere near this cairn. Round one's u = 6.7 measurement was taken in the southern half.

(2) The fix's rationale, "'west side' is doing work later ('their back to Wick')", is wrong. Wick is south (road exit 5254 'back south, to the mark'). A kneeler at a cairn on the *west* side of the road faces west and has their back to the *east*, not to Wick. The two clauses do not compose under the author's own stated geometry either, so "west side" carries no load for "back to Wick" and rewriting the line is as legitimate as moving the prop.

Also worth adding as supporting evidence the finding did not cite: comp/ninth.js:4733 already says "Her shop is off the east side of the square" against the chandler exit at x 15 of a 17-wide square, so the game's own prose uses +x = east correctly elsewhere. This line is the outlier, not a second convention.

One caveat on "the direction sense is not ambiguous in this world": it is unambiguous on the x axis (five agreeing sites) but not on y. PLACES.square's exit to the lane is at y 14.3 of h 15, the +y edge, and is named 'the lane, north' (5105), while road/mark use -y = north (5228, 5255). That inconsistency is on the other axis and does not touch this finding.

Severity: polish, not bug. Nothing behaves wrong; it is one word of flavour text contradicting prop data, with no mechanical effect. To register it as a contradiction a player has to carry a compass model built two places back (village/mark exit labels or the map screen) into a place whose own exits are labelled only north and south. Round one graded the comparable player-facing prose defect, [9] "go to back down the lane", as polish.

What v actually changes, from PAINT.house:6853-6855 and 6890-6925: thatched = v%3 !== 2 (so only v2 and v5 are slate), flip = v%2 === 1 (which way the ridge runs), rh = 26 + (v%3)*6 (roof pitch), which wall gets the bare wattle, the sill u, the tally count and a straw cross on v3.

So v4 and v0 are both thatch, both flip = false (same ridge direction, same gable facing, same wall carrying the door), and differ only by rh 32 against 26 on a 78px-tall house. That makes the north-west house, the north-centre house and the south-west house one silhouette repeated three times, and two of them (both v4) are the same variant outright, differing only in footprint (4x3.4 against 4.2x3.4) and in the per-sprite rng noise in the plaster. All three are on screen together: with the camera at (29,20) their footprint centres land at (540,118), (693,186) and (206,287).

This is not a bug in propVar. With 5 draws from 6 buckets a collision has probability 1 - 720/7776 = 91%, so it was always going to happen and nothing checks it. Grelling (4,3,1) and a3sq (4,5,0,3) both came out clean by luck. The code comment at 6854 says flip exists "so no two neighbours match", and on the square it does not deliver.

      CORRECTION: Real, and the geometry is exactly right, but two claims in the repro need correcting and one omission is worth adding.

(1) The pixel-diff evidence does not hold for all three pairs. The BITMAPS are identical - that is proven, see reasoning - but the COMPOSITED pixels are not, because drawVignette and the night light pass are applied per screen position after the blit. With a tight canopy crop (60x52 at local x -30..30, y -93..-41) the three road trees measure: t(1.5,18.4) vs t(1.3,12.2) mean abs luminance 1.09 (max 11), but t(1.5,18.4) vs t(8.1,15.2) 7.49 (max 30.1) and t(8.1,15.2) vs t(1.3,12.2) 8.56 (max 31.2), against a same-frame control (t0 vs itself shifted 34px) of 11.23. Removing the brightness offset does not close the gap (mean-centred 7.56 and 8.38), because the vignette has a spatial gradient across the box. Cause: t(8.1,15.2) lands 81px from the viewport centre while the other two land 167px and 219px out, so it keeps mean luminance 28.6 against 21.1 and 20.0. So the honest statement is "three copies of one silhouette at three brightnesses", not "three identical copies". The finding's quoted 1.66-against-15.81 is one pair generalised to all three.

(2) The road is worse than reported by one prop: its two long verge fences are also one bitmap. fence|0.5|8.4|0 for both b [0.8,23.6,0.5,8.4] and [9.7,23.6,0.5,8.4]. Road holds 10 distinct sprite keys for 13 props, not 11. (Arguably intentional for a fence, unlike a tree.)

(3) The proposed fix has a cost the finding does not price. blocked() at comp/ninth.js:6044-6051 tests the raw b[] AABB, so giving the road's trees 1.6x1.5 and 1.3x1.6 widens an already-oversized no-go box (round one [21]). And any fix that makes them distinct - footprint, variant, or index - necessarily creates the extra sprites: a tree sprite is 142x197x4 = 0.107MB, so road +0.21MB, lane +0.107MB, hollow's stones +0.147MB (118x109x4 each), about +0.46MB on top of the 12.07MB that round one already measured against SPRITE_BUDGET = 10MB. The dedup is currently paying for itself in bytes; the fix should land with the budget finding, not before it.
